{"prompt": "def compute_spawn(adjusted_fitness, previous_sizes, pop_size, min_species_size):\n        \"\"\"Compute the proper number of offspring per species (proportional to fitness).\"\"\"", "output": "\n        # Compute the total fitness\n        total_fitness = sum(adjusted_fitness)\n\n        # Compute the number of offspring per species\n        offspring_per_species = [0] * len(adjusted_fitness)\n        for i in range(len(adjusted_fitness)):\n            offspring_per_species[i] = int(adjusted_fitness[i] * pop_size / total_fitness)\n\n        # Compute the number of offspring per species, taking into account the previous", "solution": "\n        af_sum = sum(adjusted_fitness)\n\n        spawn_amounts = []\n        for af, ps in zip(adjusted_fitness, previous_sizes):\n            if af_sum > 0:\n                s = max(min_species_size, af / af_sum * pop_size)\n            else:\n                s = min_species_size\n\n            d = (s - ps) * 0.5\n            c = int(round(d))\n            spawn = ps\n            if abs(c) > 0:\n                spawn += c\n            elif d > 0:\n                spawn += 1\n            elif d < 0:\n                spawn -= 1\n\n            spawn_amounts.append(spawn)\n\n        # Normalize the spawn amounts so that the next generation is roughly\n        # the population size requested by the user.\n        total_spawn = sum(spawn_amounts)\n        norm = pop_size / total_spawn\n        spawn_amounts = [max(min_species_size, int(round(n * norm))) for n in spawn_amounts]\n\n        return spawn_amounts"}

{"prompt": "def intersection(self, other):\n        \"\"\"\n        :param: other - Line subclass\n        :return: Point subclass\n\n        Returns a Point object with the coordinates of the intersection\n        between the current line and the other line.\n\n        Will raise Parallel() if the two lines are parallel.\n        Will raise Collinear() if the two lines are collinear.\n        \"\"\"", "output": "\n        if self.is_parallel(other):\n            raise Parallel()\n        if self.is_collinear(other):\n            raise Collinear()\n\n        # Find the intersection point of the two lines\n        # by solving the system of equations\n        # (y1 - y2)x + (x2 - x1)y + x1y2 - x2y1 = 0\n        # (y1 - y2)x + (x2 - x1)y + x1y2 - x2y1 = 0\n        # (y1", "solution": "\n\n        if self.isCollinear(other):\n            msg = '{!r} and {!r} are collinear'\n            raise CollinearLines(msg.format(self, other))\n\n        d0 = self.A - self.B\n        d1 = other.A - other.B\n\n        denominator = (d0.x * d1.y) - (d0.y * d1.x)\n\n        if denominator == 0:\n            msg = '{!r} and {!r} are parallel'\n            raise ParallelLines(msg.format(self, other))\n\n        cp0 = self.A.cross(self.B)\n        cp1 = other.A.cross(other.B)\n\n        x_num = (cp0 * d1.x) - (d0.x * cp1)\n        y_num = (cp0 * d1.y) - (d0.y * cp1)\n\n        p = Point(x_num / denominator, y_num / denominator)\n\n        if p in self and p in other:\n            return p\n\n        msg = \"found point {!r} but not in {!r} and {!r}\"\n        raise ParallelLines(msg.format(p, self, other))"}

{"prompt": "def truncated_normal_log_likelihood(params, low, high, data):\n        \"\"\"Calculate the log likelihood of the truncated normal distribution.\n\n        Args:\n            params: tuple with (mean, std), the parameters under which we evaluate the model\n            low (float): the lower truncation bound\n            high (float): the upper truncation bound\n            data (ndarray): the one dimension list of data points for which we want to calculate the likelihood\n\n        Returns:\n            float: the negative log likelihood of observing the given data under the given parameters.\n                This is meant to be used in minimization routines.\n        \"\"\"", "output": "truncated normal distribution\n        \"\"\"\n        mean, std = params\n        log_likelihood = -0.5 * np.log(2 * np.pi) - np.log(std) - 0.5 * ((data - mean) / std) ** 2\n        log_likelihood = np.sum(log_likelihood)\n        log_likelihood -= np.log(np.sum(np.log(high - low)))\n        return log_likelihood\n\n    def truncated_normal_log_prior(params, low, high):\n       ", "solution": "\n        mu = params[0]\n        sigma = params[1]\n        if sigma == 0:\n            return np.inf\n        ll = np.sum(norm.logpdf(data, mu, sigma))\n        ll -= len(data) * np.log((norm.cdf(high, mu, sigma) - norm.cdf(low, mu, sigma)))\n        return -ll"}

{"prompt": "def calc_glide_ratios(dives, des, asc, glide_mask, depths, pitch_lf):\n    \"\"\"Calculate summary information on glides during dive ascent/descents\n\n    Args\n    ----\n    dives: (n,10)\n        Numpy record array with summary information of dives in sensor data\n    des: ndarray\n        Boolean mask of descents over sensor data\n    asc: ndarray\n        Boolean mask of descents over sensor data\n    glid_mask: ndarray\n        Boolean mask of glides over sensor data\n    depths: ndarray\n        Depth values at each sensor sampling\n    pitch_lf: ndarray\n        Pitch in radians over the low frequency signals of acceleration\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    gl_ratio: pandas.DataFrame\n        Dataframe of summary information of glides during dive descent/ascents\n\n        *Columns*:\n\n        * des_duration\n        * des_gl_duration\n        * des_gl_ratio\n        * des_mean_pitch\n        * des_rate\n        * asc_duration\n        * asc_gl_duration\n        * asc_gl_ratio\n        * asc_mean_pitch\n        * asc_rate\n    \"\"\"", "output": "       Boolean mask of glides over sensor data\n    depths: ndarray\n        Depths of sensor data\n    pitch_lf: ndarray\n        Pitch of sensor data\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    glides: (n,10)\n        Numpy record array with summary information of glides in sensor data\n    \"\"\"\n    # DESCENT\n        # total duration of the descent phase (s)\n        gl_ratio['des_duration'][i] = len(des_ind)\n\n        # total glide duration during the descent phase (s)\n        des_glides = numpy.where(glide_mask[des_ind])[0]\n        gl_ratio['des_gl_duration'][i] = len(des_glides)\n\n        if len(des_ind) == 0:\n            gl_ratio['des_gl_ratio'][i] = 0\n            gl_ratio['des_rate'][i] = 0\n        else:\n            # {"prompt": "def _input_parser_generator(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Coroutine (state machine) for the input parser.\n        \"\"\"", "output": "\n        while True:\n            # Get the next character.\n            char = (yield)\n\n            # If it's a newline, then we're done.\n            if char == '\\n':\n                break\n\n            # If it's a backslash, then we need to parse an escape sequence.\n            if char == '\\\\':\n                # Get the next character.\n                char = (yield)\n\n                # If it's a newline, then we're done.\n                if char == '\\n':\n                    break\n\n                # Otherwise", "solution": "\n        prefix = ''\n        retry = False\n        flush = False\n\n        while True:\n            flush = False\n\n            if retry:\n                retry = False\n            else:\n                # Get next character.\n                c = yield\n\n                if c == _Flush:\n                    flush = True\n                else:\n                    prefix += c\n\n            # If we have some data, check for matches.\n            if prefix:\n                is_prefix_of_longer_match = _IS_PREFIX_OF_LONGER_MATCH_CACHE[prefix]\n                match = self._get_match(prefix)\n\n                # Exact matches found, call handlers..\n                if (flush or not is_prefix_of_longer_match) and match:\n                    self._call_handler(match, prefix)\n                    prefix = ''\n\n                # {"prompt": "def cast(obj):\n    \"\"\"\n    Many tools love to subclass built-in types in order to implement useful\n    functionality, such as annotating the safety of a Unicode string, or adding\n    additional methods to a dict. However, cPickle loves to preserve those\n    subtypes during serialization, resulting in CallError during :meth:`call\n    <mitogen.parent.Context.call>` in the target when it tries to deserialize\n    the data.\n\n    This function walks the object graph `obj`, producing a copy with any\n    custom sub-types removed. The functionality is not default since the\n    resulting walk may be computationally expensive given a large enough graph.\n\n    See :ref:`serialization-rules` for a list of supported types.\n\n    :param obj:\n        Object to undecorate.\n    :returns:\n        Undecorated object.\n    \"\"\"", "output": "types replaced with built-in types.\n\n    :param obj:\n        Any object.\n    :returns:\n        A copy of `obj` with any custom sub-types replaced with built-in types.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(obj, dict):\n        return dict((cast(k), cast(v)) for k, v in obj.iteritems())\n    elif isinstance(obj, list):\n        return [cast(x) for x in obj]\n    elif isinstance(obj, tuple):\n        return tuple(cast(x) for x in obj", "solution": "\n    if isinstance(obj, dict):\n        return dict((cast(k), cast(v)) for k, v in iteritems(obj))\n    if isinstance(obj, (list, tuple)):\n        return [cast(v) for v in obj]\n    if isinstance(obj, PASSTHROUGH):\n        return obj\n    if isinstance(obj, mitogen.core.UnicodeType):\n        return mitogen.core.UnicodeType(obj)\n    if isinstance(obj, mitogen.core.BytesType):\n        return mitogen.core.BytesType(obj)\n\n    raise TypeError(\"Cannot serialize: %r: %r\" % (type(obj), obj))"}

which no space should be added.\n        continuation_chars = '=/:'\n        if len(completions) == 1 and completions[0][-1] not in continuation_chars:\n            if cword_prequote == '' and not completions[0].endswith(' '):\n                completions[0] += ' '\n\n        return completions"}
